RBPJ

UniProt ID: Q06330
Organism: Homo sapiens
Review Status: COMPLETE
📝 Provide Detailed Feedback

Gene Description

RBPJ (recombination signal binding protein for immunoglobulin kappa J region; also CBF1/CSL, the human Suppressor of Hairless/LAG-1 ortholog) is the sequence-specific DNA-binding transcription factor of the canonical Notch pathway. Its N-terminal (NTD) and beta-trefoil (BTD) domains bind the core motif C/TGTGGGAA; the BTD and C-terminal domain (CTD) form the surface that captures the RAM and ankyrin regions of the Notch intracellular domain (NICD). In the absence of NICD, RBPJ acts as a repressor by recruiting corepressors such as SPEN/SHARP, NCOR2/SMRT, CIR, L3MBTL3-KDM1A and CtBP/CtIP with histone deacetylase/demethylase activities. NICD binding displaces corepressors and creates a composite groove that recruits Mastermind-like coactivators (MAML1-3), forming the CSL-NICD-MAML ternary complex that activates target genes such as HES1, HEY1 and NRARP; on paired, head-to-head sites the complexes dimerize cooperatively. RBPJ is exploited by Epstein-Barr virus EBNA2/EBNA3 proteins. In development, RBPJ-dependent Notch transcription controls cardiac, vascular, hematopoietic and many other cell-fate decisions; RBPJ variants cause Adams-Oliver syndrome.

IPI
PMID:10713164
SKIP, a CBF1-associated protein, interacts with the ankyrin ... 		MODIFY
Summary: Binding of RBPJ to corepressors (NCOR2/SMRT, SPEN/SHARP, CIR, L3MBTL3, ATXN1/ATXN1L, KDM1A/LSD1).
Reason: Generic protein binding; these partners are corepressors recruited by RBPJ to repress Notch targets in the absence of NICD, so transcription corepressor binding is the informative MF.
Proposed replacements: transcription corepressor binding
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:10713164
CBF1 mediates transcriptional repression through the recruitment of an SMRT-histone deacetylase-containing corepressor complex.
PMID:16287852
In the absence of Notch, RBP-Jkappa represses Notch target genes through the recruitment of a corepressor complex.
PMID:9874765
We have examined the mechanism of CBF1-mediated repression and show that CBF1 binds to a unique corepressor, CBF1 interacting corepressor (CIR).
PMID:29030483
In the absence of NOTCH ICD, RBPJ recruits L3MBTL3 and the histone demethylase KDM1A (also known as LSD1) to the enhancers of Notch target genes, leading to H3K4me2 demethylation and to transcriptional repression.
PMID:21475249
both ATXN1 and BOAT1 bind to the promoter region of Hey1 and inhibit the transcriptional output of Notch through direct interactions with CBF1
PMID:23022380
LSD1 functions as a corepressor when associated with CSL-repressor complex and as a NOTCH1 coactivator upon Notch activation.

Core Functions

Sequence-specific DNA-binding transcription factor (CSL) that binds C/TGTGGGAA motifs in RNA polymerase II cis-regulatory regions of Notch targets; represses them via corepressors (SPEN, NCOR2, CIR, L3MBTL3-KDM1A) in the absence of signal.

Molecular Function:
DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific
Directly Involved In:
negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
Cellular Locations:
nucleus
Supporting Evidence:
  • file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-deep-research-falcon.md
    The hallmark feature of RBPJ is its ability to function as a **molecular switch** between transcriptional repression and activation, depending on the presence of Notch signaling
  • file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-uniprot.txt
    Acts as a transcriptional repressor when it is not associated with Notch proteins.
  • PMID:10713164
    CBF1 mediates transcriptional repression through the recruitment of an SMRT-histone deacetylase-containing corepressor complex.
  • PMID:9874765
    We have examined the mechanism of CBF1-mediated repression and show that CBF1 binds to a unique corepressor, CBF1 interacting corepressor (CIR).

Upon Notch activation, DNA-bound RBPJ captures NICD and MAML to form the CSL-NICD-MAML activation complex (cooperatively dimerizing on paired sites) that activates Notch target genes.

Molecular Function:
RNA polymerase II cis-regulatory region sequence-specific DNA binding
Directly Involved In:
positive regulation of transcription of Notch receptor target Notch signaling pathway
Cellular Locations:
nucleus
In Complex:
CSL-Notch-Mastermind transcription factor complex
Supporting Evidence:
  • file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-uniprot.txt
    When associated with some NICD product of Notch proteins (Notch intracellular domain), it acts as a transcriptional activator that activates transcription of Notch target genes.
  • PMID:16530044
    We report here the crystal structure of a Notch transcriptional activation complex containing the ankyrin domain of human Notch1 (ANK), the transcription factor CSL on cognate DNA, and a polypeptide from the coactivator Mastermind-like-1 (MAML-1).
  • PMID:17284587
    we establish here that cooperative formation of dimeric Notch transcription complexes on promoters with paired sites is required to activate transcription.

Suggested Questions for Experts

Q: Which RBPJ-bound enhancers are repressed at steady state in human cells, and how much of Notch target activation reflects de-repression versus active recruitment?

We are specifically interested in the primary function of the gene - for enzymes, what reaction is catalyzed, and what is the substrate specificity? For transporters, what is the substrate? For structural proteins or adapters, what is the broader structural role? For signaling molecules, what is the role in the pathway.

We are interested in where in or outside the cell the gene product carries out its function.

We are also interested in the signaling or biochemical pathways in which the gene functions. We are less interested in broad pleiotropic effects, except where these elucidate the precise role.

Include evidence where possible. We are interested in both experimental evidence as well as inference from structure, evolution, or bioinformatic analysis. Precise studies should be prioritized over high-throughput, where available.

Comprehensive Research Report: RBPJ Gene (UniProt Q06330)

Gene Identification and Overview

RBPJ (Recombination signal-binding protein for immunoglobulin kappa J region; also known as RBP-J, RBP-Jκ, CBF-1, or CSL) is the central DNA-binding transcription factor and nuclear effector of the canonical Notch signaling pathway in humans (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3). The protein belongs to the conserved CSL family (CBF1/Suppressor of Hairless/LAG-1), which is found across metazoan species and serves as the universal transcriptional mediator of Notch signals (pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 1-2).

Primary Molecular Function and DNA-Binding Properties

DNA-Binding Specificity

RBPJ functions as a sequence-specific DNA-binding transcription factor that recognizes a consensus motif typically represented as 5'-CGTGGGAA-3' or 5'-TGTGGGAA-3' (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 1-2). This DNA recognition is mediated through cooperative interactions between the N-terminal domain (NTD) and the beta-trefoil domain (BTD), which contact the major and minor grooves of DNA, respectively (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27). The BTD contains critical residues including arginine 218 (R218), which is essential for DNA binding; mutation of R218 to histidine disrupts DNA association and dramatically shortens nuclear residence time (pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 12-14).

While RBPJ exhibits sequence-specific DNA binding, genomic analysis reveals that only a subset of RBPJ binding sites contain the canonical motif, with cooperating transcription factors and chromatin context also influencing occupancy (friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 53-58, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 49-53).

Transcriptional Switching Function

The hallmark feature of RBPJ is its ability to function as a molecular switch between transcriptional repression and activation, depending on the presence of Notch signaling (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3). In the absence of Notch activation, DNA-bound RBPJ recruits corepressor complexes containing SHARP/SPEN, NCoR/SMRT, histone deacetylases (HDACs), and chromatin-modifying enzymes including KDM1A/LSD1 and L3MBTL3, thereby maintaining transcriptional repression of Notch target genes (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 5-8, sprinzak2021biophysicsofnotch pages 13-14). Following Notch receptor activation and proteolytic release of the Notch intracellular domain (NICD), NICD enters the nucleus and binds RBPJ, displacing corepressors and recruiting the coactivator Mastermind-like (MAML) to form a ternary transcriptional activation complex (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3). This complex subsequently recruits histone acetyltransferases such as p300/EP300, PCAF, and GCN5, which promote chromatin accessibility and activate transcription (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16).

Protein Structure and Domain Architecture

RBPJ exhibits a highly conserved modular architecture consisting of three principal domains integrated into a single structural unit. A detailed summary of the structural organization is provided below:

Domain Name Residues/Location Molecular Function Key Interactions
Conserved CSL structural core Approximately 425-aa conserved core within the 500-aa human RBPJ protein; organized as NTD–BTD–CTD Forms the integrated DNA-binding and transcriptional-coregulator scaffold characteristic of the CSL/Su(H) family; a β-strand spanning the domains helps unite them structurally Supports assembly of mutually regulated corepressor and NICD–MAML coactivator complexes (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, hall2022thestructurebinding pages 1-2, oswald2018cslassociatedcorepressorand pages 3-5)
N-terminal domain (NTD) N-terminal portion of the conserved core; immunoglobulin/Rel-homology-like fold Contributes an electropositive surface for sequence-specific DNA recognition, primarily contacting the major groove; also participates in assembly of the Notch transcription complex Contacts DNA, NICD ankyrin repeats, MAML, L3MBTL2, and portions of SHARP-containing complexes (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, yuan2019structuralandfunctional pages 1-3, oswald2018cslassociatedcorepressorand pages 3-5)
β-trefoil domain (BTD) Central domain; includes the DNA-contact region around Arg218 and an exposed hydrophobic pocket Cooperates with the NTD to recognize canonical RBPJ/CSL elements, typically containing the core GTGGGAA; a β-hairpin contacts the minor groove. Also serves as the principal docking site for short hydrophobic coregulator motifs Binds the NICD RAM domain through its conserved ΦWΦP motif, with reported affinity of approximately 20 nM; also binds SHARP, FHL1/KyoT2, RITA1, EBNA2, and other regulators. R218H disrupts DNA binding (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 8-12, hall2022thestructurebinding pages 1-2, oswald2018cslassociatedcorepressorand pages 3-5)
C-terminal domain (CTD) C-terminal portion of the conserved core; immunoglobulin/Rel-homology-like fold; includes functionally important Leu388 Primarily provides protein-interaction surfaces that stabilize activating or repressing transcription complexes rather than serving as the main sequence-recognition module Contacts NICD ankyrin repeats and contributes to the composite NICD–MAML interface; participates in bipartite SHARP binding and interactions with other corepressors (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 8-12, yuan2019structuralandfunctional pages 1-3)
Composite DNA-binding surface Formed mainly by the NTD and BTD; includes BTD residues Arg218, Arg220, and Ser221 Recognizes RBPJ/CSL response elements such as 5′-TGTGGGAA-3′ or the closely related 5′-CGTGGGAA-3′. The NTD and BTD contact major and minor grooves, respectively; intact DNA binding supports long-lived chromatin residence Associates with promoters and enhancers of Notch-responsive genes; DNA-binding-defective R218H markedly shortens nuclear residence time (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 12-14)
NICD/coactivator interaction surface Composite surface across the BTD, CTD, and NTD Converts DNA-bound RBPJ from a repressive platform into the Notch transcription complex: NICD RAM first engages the BTD, NICD ankyrin repeats contact NTD/CTD, and the resulting conformation permits MAML recruitment NICD1–4, MAML proteins, and downstream coactivators including p300/EP300, PCAF, and GCN5 (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, sprinzak2021biophysicsofnotch pages 13-14, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27)
Corepressor interaction surface Distributed principally across the BTD and CTD, with additional NTD contacts for selected repressors; F261 and L388 are important SHARP-interface residues Recruits chromatin-repressive machinery in the Notch-OFF state; partner-binding surfaces partly overlap with NICD-binding surfaces, supporting mutually exclusive or competitive complex assembly SHARP/SPEN, NCoR/SMRT–HDAC complexes, L3MBTL3–KDM1A/LSD1, FHL1/KyoT2, RITA1, and PRC1.6-associated L3MBTL2 (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 5-8, sprinzak2021biophysicsofnotch pages 13-14, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 8-12, yuan2019structuralandfunctional pages 1-3)
SUMO-interaction motif (SIM) NTD residues 187–191; hydrophobic residues Ile187 and Val189 are functionally important Mediates non-covalent recognition of SUMO, preferentially SUMO2/3, helping recruit or stabilize RBPJ at SUMO-rich Notch regulatory elements; this is distinct from covalent SUMOylation of RBPJ Engages SUMOylated PRC1.6 components, especially L3MBTL2; I187A/V189A reduces SUMO2 binding, and inhibition of SUMOylation decreases RBPJ chromatin occupancy (sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 1-2, sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 7-9, sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 6-7)
Covalent SUMOylation sites Lys110 and Lys195, identified in activated dendritic cells Ubc9-dependent SUMOylation protects RBPJ from ubiquitin-mediated proteasomal degradation, increasing protein stability and transcriptional activity toward CIITA and MHC-II genes Ubc9, SUMO machinery, ubiquitin–proteasome system, and CIITA promoter complexes; SUMOylation-defective RBPJ has reduced transcriptional activity (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 7-8, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 5-7)

Table: This table maps RBPJ’s conserved domains, DNA- and cofactor-binding surfaces, SUMO-interaction motif, and experimentally identified SUMOylation sites to their molecular functions. It distinguishes structural docking elements from post-translational regulatory sites.

The NTD and BTD together form the DNA-binding interface, while the BTD serves as the primary protein-interaction hub for both activating (NICD, MAML) and repressing (SHARP, FHL1/KyoT2) cofactors (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, yuan2019structuralandfunctional pages 1-3, oswald2018cslassociatedcorepressorand pages 3-5). The BTD contains an exposed hydrophobic pocket that binds the conserved ΦWΦ P (phi-tryptophan-phi-proline) motif found in the RAM region of NICD, with an affinity of approximately 20 nM (hall2022thestructurebinding pages 1-2). The C-terminal domain contributes additional interaction surfaces for NICD ankyrin repeats and participates in bipartite binding with corepressors (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, yuan2019structuralandfunctional pages 1-3).

Subcellular Localization

RBPJ is predominantly localized in the cell nucleus, where it carries out its transcriptional regulatory functions (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 12-14). The protein undergoes dynamic nucleo-cytoplasmic shuttling regulated by binding to cofactors such as Hairless in Drosophila and potentially similar mechanisms in mammals (wolf2021nucleocytoplasmicshuttlingof pages 1-2, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27). Nuclear localization is essential for RBPJ's ability to bind DNA at Notch target gene regulatory elements and assemble transcriptional complexes (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 49-53). RITA (RBPJ-interacting and tubulin-associated protein) can facilitate nuclear export of RBPJ, providing an additional layer of regulatory control (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27).

Role in Notch Signaling Pathway

RBPJ serves as the central nuclear transcriptional mediator of canonical Notch signaling. The pathway's core mechanism and RBPJ's dual regulatory functions are summarized in the following table:

Signaling State RBPJ Complex Composition Cofactors/Partners Chromatin Modifications Transcriptional Output
Notch OFF: basal repression DNA-bound RBPJ assembles a corepressor complex at canonical RBPJ/CSL response elements and serves as the sequence-specific DNA-binding scaffold. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 5-8) SHARP/SPEN, NCoR/SMRT, HDAC-containing complexes, KyoT2/FHL1, L3MBTL3–KDM1A/LSD1, and PRC1.6-associated proteins. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 5-8, sprinzak2021biophysicsofnotch pages 13-14, yuan2019structuralandfunctional pages 1-3, hall2022thestructurebinding pages 1-2) Histone deacetylation and repressive changes in histone methylation reduce promoter and enhancer activity. SUMOylated PRC1.6 components can stabilize RBPJ recruitment to chromatin. (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16, sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 1-2, sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 6-7) Basal expression of Notch-responsive genes is silenced or dampened. Repressed targets include HES1, HEY1, and context-dependent genes such as KCNN1 and LGMN. (friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 53-58, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 58-62)
Notch ON: complex exchange Ligand-induced receptor cleavage releases NICD, which enters the nucleus, binds DNA-associated RBPJ, and displaces or competes with corepressors. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3) The NICD RAM region binds RBPJ's β-trefoil domain, while NICD ankyrin repeats contact RBPJ's N- and C-terminal domains; these interactions enable MAML1–3 recruitment. (sprinzak2021biophysicsofnotch pages 13-14, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, yuan2019structuralandfunctional pages 1-3) Repressor-associated deacetylation is replaced by coactivator-driven chromatin opening. Recruitment of p300/EP300, PCAF, GCN5, and SETD1A promotes H3K27 acetylation and activating histone methylation. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 113-116, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 16-20) RBPJ is converted from a repressive scaffold into the DNA-targeting component of the Notch transcription complex; transcription begins at responsive promoters and enhancers. (sprinzak2021biophysicsofnotch pages 13-14, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 113-116)
Notch ON: productive activation The ternary RBPJ–NICD–MAML complex remains tethered to RBPJ response elements and recruits additional transcriptional and chromatin-remodeling machinery. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16) MAML proteins, p300/EP300, PCAF, GCN5, SETD1A, and context-specific transcription factors or coactivators. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 23-27, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 16-20) Increased chromatin accessibility and activating histone marks—especially H3K27ac—support enhancer activation and transcription. Dynamic distal RBPJ sites and super-enhancers are particularly Notch-responsive. (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 113-116) Canonical targets such as HES1, HEY1/HEY-family genes, MYC, NRARP, and DTX1/Deltex are activated, regulating cell fate, differentiation, proliferation, and feedback control. (pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 1-2, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3)
Signal termination/reset NICD turnover dismantles the activation complex, allowing RBPJ to reassociate with corepressors and restore the Notch-OFF state. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 13-16) Loss of NICD/MAML is followed by renewed recruitment of SHARP/SPEN, NCoR/SMRT, HDACs, and other repressors; RITA-mediated export can also reduce nuclear RBPJ availability. (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 5-8, sprinzak2021biophysicsofnotch pages 13-14) Coactivator-dependent acetylation declines, while deacetylation and repressive chromatin regulation are restored. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 5-8) Notch-induced transcription is attenuated, limiting response amplitude and duration and re-establishing basal repression. (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 3-5, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 1-2)

Table: This table summarizes how RBPJ switches between corepressor and coactivator complexes during canonical Notch signaling. It links each pathway state to specific partners, chromatin effects, and representative transcriptional targets.

Key Target Genes

RBPJ regulates a well-characterized set of Notch target genes, including the basic helix-loop-helix transcription factors HES1 and HEY1/HEY family members, the proto-oncogene c-MYC, and feedback regulators such as NRARP and DTX1/Deltex (pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 1-2, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 53-58, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 58-62, giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3). Chromatin immunoprecipitation studies have also identified KCNN1, LGMN, IL2RA, and CIITA as direct RBPJ targets in specific cellular contexts (friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 53-58, friedrich2023decipheringthenotch pages 58-62, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 7-8).

Biological Processes and Broader Signaling Roles

Beyond canonical Notch signaling, RBPJ regulates diverse biological processes:

  1. Immune Cell Development and Function: RBPJ is essential for T-cell development, hematopoietic homeostasis, and lymphocyte differentiation (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3, chen2024theexpressionof pages 9-11). It regulates dendritic cell antigen presentation through control of CIITA and MHC class II expression (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 7-8, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 1-2).

  2. Inflammatory Signaling: RBPJ modulates macrophage inflammatory responses by suppressing TGFβ receptor expression and thereby amplifying TNF-driven inflammation (xia2024reciprocalsuppressionbetween pages 9-11). It also regulates Jagged1 expression to create negative feedback on TNF-mediated osteoclastogenesis (ng2023jagged1actsas pages 1-3, ng2023jagged1actsas pages 4-6).

  3. Cancer Biology: RBPJ has context-dependent roles in cancer, functioning as both a tumor suppressor and oncogenic factor depending on tissue type (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 1-2, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, borggrefe2016settingthestage pages 7-8).

Recent Developments (2023-2024)

Recent research has revealed novel regulatory mechanisms and therapeutic implications for RBPJ:

Study/Year Main Finding Biological Context Clinical Relevance Key Mechanism
Pan et al., 2023 High RBPJ was associated with PD-1⁺, TIM-3⁺, granzyme-B-low exhausted CD8⁺ T cells in hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC). RBPJ knockdown or inhibition of the RBPJ transcriptional complex with RIN1 reduced exhaustion markers and improved antitumor activity. (pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 2-5, pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 1-2) Tumor-infiltrating CD8⁺ T cells and HCC cells; patient datasets, mouse models, and cell-based experiments Supports RBPJ-complex inhibition as a candidate strategy to reduce immune exhaustion and enhance anti-PD-1/PD-L1 therapy; RIN1 also suppressed tumor-cell PD-L1, L-kynurenine production, and mTOR signaling. Evidence remains preclinical. RBPJ-associated transcription promotes inhibitory-receptor expression and an exhausted T-cell state; RIN1 disrupts this program, while RBPJ knockdown lowers PD-1 and TIM-3. (pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 2-5, pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 1-2)
Zhang et al., 2023 Ubc9-dependent SUMOylation of RBPJ at K110 and K195 stabilized the protein, enhanced its transcriptional activity, and increased CIITA and MHC class II expression in dendritic cells. (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 7-8, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 5-7) LPS-activated dendritic cells and a mouse DSS-induced colitis model Identifies the Ubc9–RBPJ–CIITA axis as a potential inflammatory-disease target. Dendritic-cell Ubc9 deficiency impaired antigen presentation and CD4⁺ T-cell priming and reduced colitis severity. SUMOylation protects RBPJ from ubiquitin-mediated proteasomal degradation; stabilized RBPJ binds the CIITA promoter and promotes the MHC-II antigen-presentation program. (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 7-8, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 1-2)
Ng et al., 2023 Jagged1 was identified as a direct RBPJ target that feeds back to suppress TNF-driven inflammatory osteoclastogenesis. Recombinant Jagged1 inhibited human inflammatory osteoclast formation without broadly suppressing TNF-induced inflammatory genes. (ng2023jagged1actsas pages 1-3, ng2023jagged1actsas pages 4-6) Macrophages and osteoclast precursors exposed to TNF; mouse and human cultures, with comparison of rheumatoid arthritis and systemic lupus erythematosus samples Suggests that restoring Jagged1 signaling could limit inflammatory bone loss, including rheumatoid-arthritis-associated erosion, while potentially preserving broader immune responses. TNF induces RBPJ-dependent JAG1 transcription; Jagged1 then establishes negative feedback that restrains TNF-mediated—but not RANKL-mediated—osteoclast differentiation. (ng2023jagged1actsas pages 1-3, ng2023jagged1actsas pages 4-6)
Xia et al., 2024 RBPJ amplified TNF-driven macrophage inflammation by suppressing homeostatic TGFβ signaling. RBPJ loss reduced TNF-induced cytokines and interferon-stimulated genes and lowered circulating inflammatory mediators in mice. (xia2024reciprocalsuppressionbetween pages 9-11) Macrophage inflammatory responses and a TNF-induced chronic-inflammation mouse model Positions RBPJ-mediated repression of TGFβ signaling as a possible intervention point in TNF-dependent inflammatory disease; therapeutic translation has not yet been established. RBPJ binds the TGFBR1 and TGFBR2 promoters and represses receptor expression, weakening TGFβ-mediated restraint of TNF responses; RBPJ may also directly regulate inflammatory genes. (xia2024reciprocalsuppressionbetween pages 9-11)
Chen et al., 2024 RBPJ mRNA was lower in PBMCs from 83 newly diagnosed rheumatoid arthritis patients than in 70 healthy controls and correlated inversely with DAS28, CRP, and ESR. Synovial analysis identified 562 genes differing between RBPJ-low and RBPJ-high groups. (chen2024theexpressionof pages 9-11, chen2024theexpressionof pages 1-2) Human rheumatoid arthritis PBMCs and the GSE89408 synovial-tissue transcriptomic dataset Suggests RBPJ as a candidate disease-activity biomarker and mechanistic link to immune dysregulation and bone destruction, but the observational design does not establish causality or clinical utility. RBPJ-associated genes were enriched in T-cell-receptor signaling, Th1/Th2 and Th17 differentiation, PI3K–AKT and cytokine signaling, lymphocyte differentiation, and osteoclastogenesis. (chen2024theexpressionof pages 9-11, chen2024theexpressionof pages 1-2)

Table: Summary of major 2023–2024 studies linking RBPJ to tumor immunity, post-translational regulation, antigen presentation, inflammatory signaling, rheumatoid arthritis, and osteoclastogenesis. The table distinguishes mechanistic findings from still-preclinical or associative clinical implications.

Post-Translational Modifications

A major 2023 discovery identified Ubc9-mediated SUMOylation of RBPJ at lysines K110 and K195 as a critical regulatory mechanism (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 7-8, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 1-2, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 5-7). SUMOylation protects RBPJ from ubiquitin-mediated proteasomal degradation, thereby stabilizing the protein and enhancing its transcriptional activity (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 7-8, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 5-7). This modification is particularly important in dendritic cells, where it sustains CIITA expression and MHC class II antigen presentation (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 1-2, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 2-3).

Additionally, RBPJ contains a SUMO-interaction motif (SIM) at residues 187-191 in the N-terminal domain that enables non-covalent recognition of SUMOylated PRC1.6 complex components, particularly L3MBTL2, facilitating RBPJ recruitment to chromatin (sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 1-2, sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 7-9, sotomska2021sumoylatednoncanonicalpolycomb pages 6-7). Phosphorylation of RBPJ-associated corepressors, particularly casein kinase II (CKII)-dependent phosphorylation of NCoR, regulates the balance between repressive and activating complexes (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 5-8).

Cancer Immunotherapy Applications

In 2023, Pan and colleagues identified RBPJ as a driver of CD8+ T-cell exhaustion in hepatocellular carcinoma, with high RBPJ expression correlating with increased PD-1, TIM-3, and reduced granzyme B (pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 2-5, pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 1-2). Pharmacological inhibition of the RBPJ transcriptional complex using the small molecule RIN1 reduced T-cell exhaustion markers, inhibited tumor growth through dual effects on mTOR signaling and PD-L1 expression, and synergized with anti-PD-1/PD-L1 checkpoint blockade (pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 2-5, pan2023targetedinhibitionof pages 1-2). These findings position RBPJ as a potential immunotherapy target for overcoming resistance in HCC.

Inflammatory Disease

A 2024 study demonstrated that RBPJ expression is significantly reduced in peripheral blood mononuclear cells from rheumatoid arthritis patients and inversely correlates with disease activity scores (DAS28), C-reactive protein, and erythrocyte sedimentation rate (chen2024theexpressionof pages 9-11, chen2024theexpressionof pages 1-2). Differential gene expression analysis linked RBPJ to T-cell activation, Th17 differentiation, osteoclastogenesis, and inflammatory signaling pathways, suggesting that diminished RBPJ may contribute to immune dysregulation and joint destruction in RA (chen2024theexpressionof pages 9-11, chen2024theexpressionof pages 1-2, chen2024theexpressionof pages 11-12).

Clinical Relevance and Disease Associations

RBPJ dysfunction has been implicated in multiple human diseases:

  1. Developmental Disorders: Heterozygous RBPJ mutations cause Adams-Oliver syndrome, characterized by limb defects, aplasia cutis, and vascular abnormalities (hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 20-23).

  2. Hematologic Malignancies: Aberrant RBPJ-corepressor activity may contribute to leukemogenesis, particularly in T-cell acute lymphoblastic leukemia (T-ALL), where Notch1 mutations are frequent (giaimo2021transcriptionfactorrbpj pages 1-3, borggrefe2016settingthestage pages 7-8).

  3. Solid Tumors: RBPJ has been implicated in pancreatic cancer, breast cancer, and other malignancies, with context-dependent tumor-suppressive or oncogenic roles (pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 1-2, hoffmeister2024singlemoleculetracking pages 23-27, pan2021transcriptionfactorrbpjl pages 8-12).

  4. Inflammatory Bowel Disease: The Ubc9-RBPJ-CIITA axis in dendritic cells promotes DSS-induced colitis, suggesting potential therapeutic targeting for inflammatory bowel diseases (zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 1-2, zhang2023ubc9regulatesthe pages 2-3).

  5. Bone Diseases: RBPJ regulates osteoclastogenesis and inflammatory bone loss, with relevance to rheumatoid arthritis and metabolic bone disease (ng2023jagged1actsas pages 1-3, ng2023jagged1actsas pages 4-6).

Conclusions

RBPJ is a highly conserved, sequence-specific DNA-binding transcription factor that serves as the obligate nuclear mediator of canonical Notch signaling. Its unique ability to switch between transcriptional repression and activation, mediated through dynamic assembly of distinct corepressor and coactivator complexes, positions it as a central regulator of cell fate, differentiation, and immune function. Recent discoveries have expanded our understanding of RBPJ regulation through post-translational modifications, particularly SUMOylation, and identified novel therapeutic opportunities in cancer immunotherapy and inflammatory disease. The protein's nuclear localization, well-defined domain architecture, and precisely characterized DNA-binding specificity make it an excellent model system for understanding context-dependent transcriptional control and a promising target for therapeutic intervention in Notch-dependent diseases.

📚 Additional Documentation

Notes

(RBPJ-notes.md)

RBPJ (human, Q06330) review notes

Identity

  • CSL / CBF1 / Su(H) ortholog. PANTHER PTHR10665 (RECOMBINING BINDING PROTEIN SUPPRESSOR OF HAIRLESS).
  • IBA nodes: DNA-binding TF activity, cis-regulatory DNA binding, nucleus PTN000071433; Notch signaling pathway and MAML1-RBP-Jkappa-ICN1 complex PTN002580211.

Key findings

  • Repressor by default: binds CIR PMID:9874765, SMRT/NCOR2 PMID:10713164, SHARP/SPEN -> CtIP/CtBP PMID:16287852, L3MBTL3-KDM1A PMID:29030483.
  • Activator with NICD and MAML: crystal structure of CSL-ANK-MAML1 on DNA PMID:16530044; cooperative dimers on paired sites PMID:17284587.
  • RITA1 exports RBPJ from nucleus PMID:21102556.
  • Pharmacological disruption of the complex (SAHM1 PMID:19907488, CB-103 PMID:32601208).
  • COX4I2 oxygen responsive element activation PMID:23303788.
  • EBV EBNA2/EBNA3 target RBPJ PMID:8627785.

Decisions

  • Core: GO:0000981 DNA-binding TF activity RNAPII-specific; GO:0000978; complexes GO:1990433/GO:0002193; processes GO:0000122, GO:0007221, GO:0007219.
  • protein binding: NICD/MAML1/SNW1 -> transcription coactivator binding (GO:0001223); corepressors -> transcription corepressor binding (GO:0001222); viral, p53, KCTD1, RITA1, AP-MS hits -> REMOVE.
  • GO:0061629 with SPEN -> MODIFY to corepressor binding.
  • Mouse cardiac/vascular development terms non-core; BMP/ERBB/ephrin positive regulation and "regulation of generation of precursor metabolites" over-annotated.

Variant-relevant biology

  • CSL is the single DNA-binding effector; "default repression" mode; repressor partners differ by lineage (SPEN/SHARP in mammals vs Hairless in Drosophila).

Deep research

  • Falcon deep research completed: file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-deep-research-falcon.md (first attempt timed out at the 600 s wrapper default; rerun with --timeout 2700). Key statements quoted in the review are verbatim from that file.

id: Q06330
gene_symbol: RBPJ
product_type: PROTEIN
status: COMPLETE
taxon:
  id: NCBITaxon:9606
  label: Homo sapiens
description: >-
  RBPJ (recombination signal binding protein for immunoglobulin kappa J region; also CBF1/CSL,
  the human Suppressor of Hairless/LAG-1 ortholog) is the sequence-specific DNA-binding transcription
  factor of the canonical Notch pathway. Its N-terminal (NTD) and beta-trefoil (BTD) domains
  bind the core motif C/TGTGGGAA; the BTD and C-terminal domain (CTD) form the surface that
  captures the RAM and ankyrin regions of the Notch intracellular domain (NICD). In the absence
  of NICD, RBPJ acts as a repressor by recruiting corepressors such as SPEN/SHARP, NCOR2/SMRT,
  CIR, L3MBTL3-KDM1A and CtBP/CtIP with histone deacetylase/demethylase activities. NICD binding
  displaces corepressors and creates a composite groove that recruits Mastermind-like coactivators
  (MAML1-3), forming the CSL-NICD-MAML ternary complex that activates target genes such as
  HES1, HEY1 and NRARP; on paired, head-to-head sites the complexes dimerize cooperatively.
  RBPJ is exploited by Epstein-Barr virus EBNA2/EBNA3 proteins. In development, RBPJ-dependent
  Notch transcription controls cardiac, vascular, hematopoietic and many other cell-fate decisions;
  RBPJ variants cause Adams-Oliver syndrome.

    - reference_id: PMID:9874765
      supporting_text: >-
        We have examined the mechanism of CBF1-mediated repression and show that CBF1 binds
        to a unique corepressor, CBF1 interacting corepressor (CIR).
    - reference_id: PMID:29030483
      supporting_text: >-
        In the absence of NOTCH ICD, RBPJ recruits L3MBTL3 and the histone demethylase KDM1A
        (also known as LSD1) to the enhancers of Notch target genes, leading to H3K4me2 demethylation
        and to transcriptional repression.
    - reference_id: PMID:21475249
      supporting_text: >-
        both ATXN1 and BOAT1 bind to the promoter region of Hey1 and inhibit the transcriptional
        output of Notch through direct interactions with CBF1
    - reference_id: PMID:23022380
      supporting_text: >-
        LSD1 functions as a corepressor when associated with CSL-repressor complex and as
        a NOTCH1 coactivator upon Notch activation.

- id: file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-uniprot.txt
  title: UniProtKB entry Q06330 (SUHH_HUMAN / RBPJ)
  findings: []
core_functions:
- description: >-
    Sequence-specific DNA-binding transcription factor (CSL) that binds C/TGTGGGAA motifs
    in RNA polymerase II cis-regulatory regions of Notch targets; represses them via corepressors
    (SPEN, NCOR2, CIR, L3MBTL3-KDM1A) in the absence of signal.
  molecular_function:
    id: GO:0000981
    label: DNA-binding transcription factor activity, RNA polymerase II-specific
  directly_involved_in:
  - id: GO:0000122
    label: negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
  locations:
  - id: GO:0005634
    label: nucleus
  supported_by:
  - reference_id: file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-deep-research-falcon.md
    supporting_text: >-
      The hallmark feature of RBPJ is its ability to function as a **molecular switch** between
      transcriptional repression and activation, depending on the presence of Notch signaling
  - reference_id: file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-uniprot.txt
    supporting_text: >-
      Acts as a transcriptional repressor when it is not associated with Notch proteins.
  - reference_id: PMID:10713164
    supporting_text: >-
      CBF1 mediates transcriptional repression through the recruitment of an SMRT-histone
      deacetylase-containing corepressor complex.
  - reference_id: PMID:9874765
    supporting_text: >-
      We have examined the mechanism of CBF1-mediated repression and show that CBF1 binds
      to a unique corepressor, CBF1 interacting corepressor (CIR).
- description: >-
    Upon Notch activation, DNA-bound RBPJ captures NICD and MAML to form the CSL-NICD-MAML
    activation complex (cooperatively dimerizing on paired sites) that activates Notch target
    genes.
  molecular_function:
    id: GO:0000978
    label: RNA polymerase II cis-regulatory region sequence-specific DNA binding
  directly_involved_in:
  - id: GO:0007221
    label: positive regulation of transcription of Notch receptor target
  - id: GO:0007219
    label: Notch signaling pathway
  locations:
  - id: GO:0005634
    label: nucleus
  in_complex:
    id: GO:1990433
    label: CSL-Notch-Mastermind transcription factor complex
  supported_by:
  - reference_id: file:human/RBPJ/RBPJ-uniprot.txt
    supporting_text: >-
      When associated with some NICD product of Notch proteins (Notch intracellular domain),
      it acts as a transcriptional activator that activates transcription of Notch target
      genes.
  - reference_id: PMID:16530044
    supporting_text: >-
      We report here the crystal structure of a Notch transcriptional activation complex containing
      the ankyrin domain of human Notch1 (ANK), the transcription factor CSL on cognate DNA,
      and a polypeptide from the coactivator Mastermind-like-1 (MAML-1).
  - reference_id: PMID:17284587
    supporting_text: >-
      we establish here that cooperative formation of dimeric Notch transcription complexes
      on promoters with paired sites is required to activate transcription.
